#2945. LeBron Log No. 4: A courtside sip, 16,000 makes and Cleveland's welcome

LeBron Log No. 4: A courtside sip, 16,000 makes and Cleveland's welcome originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

LeBron James' latest week with the Sixers included a sip of a stranger's wine, yet another number no NBA player had reached and a return to the city where his career began.

It also included his worst performance with Philadelphia, followed less than 48 hours later by his highest-scoring game with the team.

Welcome to the fourth edition of the LeBron Log.

No chronicle of James' first season in Philadelphia could omit the time he helped himself to a fan's wine during a game.

James chased Tyrese Maxey's transition pass into the stands during the third quarter of the Sixers' Nov. 2 win over the Bulls. He collided with Philadelphia native Troy Clemons, spilled some of Clemons' wine and sampled what had landed on his arm.

The interaction received its own story on our site, and deservedly so. It also belongs in the Log.

Clemons did not keep the cup as a souvenir. He threw it away and got another drink.

James returned to the court and later broke into a dance after scoring over the 6-foot-1 Tre Jones. The wine clip generated millions of views across social media and became the defining image from an otherwise ordinary 19-point Sixers victory.

The following night in Detroit, James offered a broader explanation for why Philadelphia has already looked comfortable together.

"We've got a good team," James said. "Guys vibe with one another. Guys like each other. Guys enjoy being around each other. That has a lot to do with it, too. It makes the game a lot easier when you enjoy being around people."

Eleven games into his Sixers career, James appears to be doing exactly that.

James arrived in Detroit two baskets away from another unprecedented number.

His second field goal against the Pistons made him the first player in NBA history to reach 16,000 regular-season field goals.

James finished the Sixers' 112-106 victory with 13 points, 10 rebounds and eight assists. The near-triple-double helped Philadelphia improve to 8-1 and avenge its only loss of the season.

Kareem Abdul-Jabbar ranks second in league history with 15,837 made field goals. Karl Malone is a distant third at 13,528. James is the only active player with more than 12,000.

His scoring record has long been secure. James passed Abdul-Jabbar in career points in February 2023 and later overtook him in made baskets. The round number in Detroit still illustrated the distance he has created between himself and every other scorer.

James began his career by making 5,361 field goals across seven seasons with Cleveland. He added 3,510 in Miami, returned to make 3,691 more for the Cavaliers and made another 3,421 during eight years with the Lakers.

Basket No. 16,000 belonged to Philadelphia.

The Sixers' 8-1 start also carried a notable James connection. Each of his previous four teams to begin a season at least 7-1 reached the NBA Finals.

James was not ready to entertain that conversation in early November.

"It's too early to be talking about the Finals and things of that nature," he said. "We've got to continue to buy into what we want to do."

Two nights later, James returned to the place where nearly half of those 16,000 baskets were made.

His first game in Cleveland as a Sixer brought out jerseys from every stop of his career. Cavaliers colors filled Rocket Arena alongside Heat black, Lakers purple and the Sixers' throwback look.

James received a warm ovation when he was introduced last among Philadelphia's starters. When he checked out late in the fourth quarter, with the result decided, the crowd stood for him again.

James has experienced both ends of Cleveland's emotional range.

He returned as a member of the Heat on Dec. 2, 2010, to one of the most hostile receptions in NBA history. Six years later, he delivered the Cavaliers their first championship and ended Cleveland's 52-year major professional sports title drought.

"I've seen the depths of hell when it comes to a reception here, all the way to the heavens above," James said. "Anything in between is easy money."

The reception fell somewhere in the middle. His performance landed much closer to the bottom.

James finished with eight points on 3-for-12 shooting, three assists and six turnovers in the Sixers' 119-95 loss. It was his first single-digit performance in Cleveland since he scored three points against the Rockets on Dec. 30, 2004.

He had scored at least 10 points in 396 consecutive regular-season games in the city. The run covered his first stint with the Cavaliers, every visit as an opponent and his entire second tenure with the organization.

It ended during his first trip back in a Sixers uniform.

"I definitely did not feel in a good rhythm offensively," James said. "A couple of them were careless. A couple of them were just trying to make the right play and not being successful with it."

It was only the 10th single-digit regular-season game of James' 1,630-game career. He still took time afterward to consider what an eventual jersey retirement in Cleveland could represent.

"The journey from being an 18-year-old rookie, to leading a franchise to a championship," James said. "And everything in between."

James did not carry the rough night into Chicago.

Less than 48 hours after struggling in Cleveland, he scored a season-high 22 points in the Sixers' 127-121 victory over the Bulls. Thirteen came during the second quarter, when James helped Philadelphia turn an early deficit into a halftime lead.

His first basket produced another milestone.

James entered Sunday trailing Abdul-Jabbar's 1,895 points for the most scored against the Bulls in NBA history. His first-quarter three-pointer moved him into the lead. He ended the afternoon with 1,916.

The most interesting play from his night was a pass.

With the Sixers ahead by four and 2:20 remaining, James drove into the paint. Three Chicago defenders collapsed toward him. Maxey stood between the left wing and corner, but even he did not believe James could see him.

James fired the ball through the defense. Maxey sidestepped Isaac Okoro and made the three-pointer, pushing Philadelphia's lead to seven.

After his postgame interview, James pulled out his phone and watched the play again.

"He didn't think I saw him," James called out from his locker as reporters asked Maxey about the possession.

Maxey acknowledged that he had positioned himself to rebound because he expected James to shoot.

"Honestly, I was like this," Maxey said, raising his hands. "I thought he was about to shoot a layup."

James has spent the opening weeks of the season teaching his new teammates to expect the unexpected pass.

"You've got to become a wide receiver or a catcher at that point," Maxey said. "You never know when you're going to get the ball.

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 2:00 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Productivity growth is strong, and capital investment is robust. Job gains have kept pace with the workforce, and the unemployment rate has changed little.

Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

Voting against the monetary policy action were Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack, who preferred to raise the target range for the federal funds rate by 1/4 percentage point at this meeting.

#2940. Observations after Sixers suffer blowout loss to Cavs, fall in NBA Cup

Observations after Sixers suffer blowout loss to Cavs, fall in NBA Cup originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers couldn't rekindle the magic of their comeback home-opening win over the Cavaliers.

They fell Friday night in NBA Cup action, suffering a 119-95 defeat in Cleveland. The Sixers dipped to 8-2 and the Cavs improved to 5-4.

The Sixers' top scorers were Jaylen Brown (20 points), VJ Edgecombe (19 points) and Tyrese Maxey (17).

The Cavs got 23 points from Donovan Mitchell and 22 from Evan Mobley.

The Sixers' injured players included center Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain). Cleveland was down big man Jarrett Allen (right knee contusion). Sam Merrill sat out the second half with a right elbow injury.

With Friday's loss, the Sixers are 1-1 in Eastern Conference Group B of the NBA Cup. Their next Cup game is scheduled for Nov. 25 against the Heat.

The Sixers will conclude their three-road trip Sunday afternoon against the Bulls. Here are observations on their blowout loss to Cleveland:

After a missed Merrill three-pointer, the Sixers steamed ahead on the fast break and Edgecombe opened the scoring with a slam.

The Cavs replied with a 15-0 run kickstarted by six straight Mitchell points. Much like in the Sixers' Oct. 22 win over Cleveland, they were many levels below their best in the first quarter.

The Sixers began 0 for 4 from three-point range, including two LeBron James misses. The Cavs were also the better team in transition and limited the Sixers' chances to attack in the open floor by taking excellent care of the ball in the early going. Edgecombe was called for his second foul with 7:34 left in the first quarter.

Meanwhile, Joel Embiid expressed his frustration about a couple of early no-calls. Embiid's larger problem was that he couldn't hit any sort of shot besides free throws.

He went 0 for 9 from the floor in the game. The seven-time All-Star finished with six points and seven rebounds.

Embiid has had a subpar shooting start to the year, especially from three-point territory — he's now 3 for 28 (10.7 percent) — but Friday was the kind of extreme night that's bound to happen here and there over the course of a long season.

Of course, it's notable that Embiid's been playing through right foot plantar fasciitis and the Sixers have been keeping a close eye on his minutes. We'll see how the team manages his availability moving forward and whether Embiid is able to bounce back next time out.

James didn't play anywhere near his finest game either. He had eight points on 3-for-12 shooting, eight rebounds, three assists and six turnovers.

As a team, the Sixers were 36.5 percent from the field and 25 percent beyond the arc.

Jabari Walker rejoined the Sixers' rotation as the backup center with Bona sidelined. Anfernee Simons, former Cav Dean Wade and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope all remained rotation fixtures.

With Walker and Caldwell-Pope on the court next to three starters, the Sixers cut their deficit to single digits in the second quarter. Walker threw down a fast-break dunk that cut Cleveland's lead to 48-41. He gave the Sixers some good defensive possessions when switched on to James Harden and provided his usual physical, high-effort work on the glass.

Edgecombe was fantastic in the second, sinking three triples, racking up 15 points and nabbing three steals.

Many second-year players would have a tough time finding a rhythm after encountering early foul trouble, but Edgecombe was great as soon as he checked back in and delivered a ton of valuable buckets in the Sixers' comeback push.

The Sixers pulled to within 66-63 when Maxey picked a steal and threw a pretty one-handed bounce pass to Edgecombe for a dunk.

With Embiid and James offering little as scorers, Brown hit several shots that helped the Sixers stay in the game. After his 30-point performance last time out against the Pistons, Brown reached the 20-point mark for a third consecutive game.

Still, the Sixers' offense never got rolling. The Cavs maintained their lead and extended it late in the third quarter. By early in the fourth, the Sixers trailed by over 20 points.

Embiid didn't play the fourth quarter and logged 25 minutes. He's had zero made field goals on just one other occasion in his career, back in Nov. 2019 against the Raptors.

Even with their tremendous first nine games, the Sixers knew they'd hit speed bumps throughout the year. They'll try to get back on track Sunday in Chicago.

#2938. Kingdom Keys: 3 Keys to a Chiefs victory over the Falcons in Week 10

The Kansas City Chiefs got back on track last week with a convincing 35-12 victory over the New York Jets. Now, they will try to build on that performance when they travel to face the Atlanta Falcons on Sunday afternoon.

Kansas City enters the matchup at 6-2, while Atlanta has fallen to 3-6 after consecutive losses. However, the Falcons remain within striking distance in the NFC South and possess one of the league's most dangerous offensive players in running back Bijan Robinson.

Here are three Kingdom Keys to a Chiefs victory.

Atlanta's defense has struggled to stop the run recently, allowing a league-high 190 rushing yards per game over its last three contests. That makes Kenneth Walker III an obvious focal point for Kansas City after he produced 143 yards from scrimmage and three touchdowns against the Jets.

It also means the Falcons should enter the game determined to make someone other than Walker beat them.

The Chiefs' wide receivers must provide that answer.

During Kansas City's 20-3 loss to the Seattle Seahawks, its wide receivers combined for only six receptions and 39 yards. The Seahawks crowded the line of scrimmage, slowed Walker and trusted their defensive backs to win their individual matchups.

The production improved against the Denver Broncos with Rashee Rice's return; the group combined for 13 receptions, 90 yards, and one touchdown. Last week against New York, Rice, Xavier Worthy and Jalen Royals combined for 13 catches and 115 yards. It was another step forward, but much of the production came after the catch rather than from consistently winning downfield.

That is where Kansas City's newest addition could help. The Chiefs acquired wide receiver Josh Downs from the Indianapolis Colts before the trade deadline. Downs arrives as a target who can create separation and find openings in zone coverage.

Downs should not be expected to master the entire offense in a few days, but the Chiefs can give him a defined package of plays. More importantly, Rice, Worthy and the rest of the receiving corps must win when Atlanta commits extra defenders to the run.

The goal should be at least 150 combined receiving yards from Kansas City's wide receivers in a performance that should be able to take advantage of the attention on Walker.

There is no mystery surrounding Atlanta's offensive plan: get the football in Robinson's hands.

Robinson has rushed for a league-leading 880 yards and five touchdowns while averaging 5.2 yards per carry. He has also caught 31 passes for 250 yards, giving him 1,130 yards from scrimmage through nine games. Last week against the Cincinnati Bengals, he produced 147 scrimmage yards on 25 touches.

With backup Brian Robinson Jr. on the Reserve/Injured list, the Falcons could lean even more on Bijan Robinson. He is not only their primary runner but also a dangerous receiving option who can create mismatches against linebackers.

Kansas City's defense is equipped for the challenge. The Chiefs have allowed 780 rushing yards through eight games — 97.5 yards per contest — and only 3.6 yards per carry. They have also surrendered just two runs of 20 or more yards.

The defensive line must control the line of scrimmage without creating cutback lanes, while the linebackers must tackle cleanly in space. Robinson is too talented to expect Kansas City to eliminate him entirely, but the Chiefs cannot allow him to consistently create manageable third downs.

Atlanta has converted only 37 of its 106 third-down opportunities, a 34.9% rate. If Kansas City can limit Robinson on early downs and force Michael Penix Jr. into third-and-long, Steve Spagnuolo can turn loose the pass rush that recorded five sacks against the Jets.

Keeping Robinson below 100 yards from scrimmage would put the Falcons' offense in an uncomfortable position and force Penix to carry the game.

This week's X-factor is simple: Harrison Butker must deliver.

Butker has converted only 12 of his 19 field-goal attempts this season, a career-low rate of 63.2%. He is just 2-for-8 from at least 50 yards and has missed a field goal in four consecutive games. Over that four-game stretch, he has made only five of his 10 attempts.

The Chiefs overcame his missed 51-yard attempt against the Jets because they controlled the second half. They cannot assume they will have the same margin for error on the road against Atlanta.

Head coach Andy Reid has made it clear that Butker remains Kansas City's kicker. Special teams coordinator Dave Toub has also expressed confidence in the veteran, saying the goal is to get one perfect game and begin building a streak from there.

Kansas City does not need Butker to rescue the offense with repeated attempts from beyond 50 yards. It does need him to convert the manageable opportunities he receives. In a road game that could remain close into the fourth quarter, leaving three or four points on the field could change the outcome.

The Chiefs should be able to run the football against an Atlanta defense that has struggled in recent weeks, but the Falcons will likely load the box and force Kansas City's wide receivers to prove they can win. Rice, Worthy and the newly acquired Downs must make Atlanta pay for committing extra defenders to Walker.

Defensively, Kansas City must contain Robinson on early downs and force Penix to operate in obvious passing situations. On special teams, Butker needs a clean performance that begins rebuilding his confidence.

If the Chiefs accomplish those three things, they should leave Atlanta with their second consecutive victory and remain firmly in the AFC West race

→ Baca artikel asli

#2935. ‘Let's check the ball up': Jaylen Brown ready for first game against Celtics

'Let's check the ball up': Jaylen Brown ready for first game against Celtics originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Jaylen Brown was asked Sunday whether he and Jayson Tatum had spoken since the former teammates addressed their relationship during the preseason.

Does he plan to approach Tatum before Tuesday night's game at Xfinity Mobile Arena?

"I mean, he's on his side, I'm on my side," Brown said. "We're playing basketball. Both of us are trying to win. So it's nothing to talk about. Let's check the ball up."

After months of public comments, documentaries and questions about how their partnership ended, the basketball will finally take over.

Brown will face Boston for the first time since the Celtics traded him to Philadelphia for Paul George and four future draft assets in July. He spent his first 10 NBA seasons in Boston, played nine alongside Tatum and helped the Celtics win the 2024 championship, earning Finals MVP honors.

Tuesday's 8 p.m. game on NBC will also bring together the top two teams in the Eastern Conference. The Sixers are 9-2. Boston, riding a three-game winning streak, is 8-2 and sits a half-game behind Philadelphia.

"Another good opportunity for our team to be able to show what we're made of," Brown said. "I'm looking forward to it. Big-time matchup, two heavyweights in the East. Let's get it."

Brown has not hidden his appreciation for Boston, even as more has emerged about the deterioration of his relationship with Tatum and the organization.

"I love Boston," he said Sunday. "Boston's a great city. A lot of people I developed relationships with, you know what I mean? But I'm just focused on what's in front of me right now. And that's the most important thing."

He also acknowledged everything that accompanies an abrupt end to a decade with one organization.

"Decisions were made," Brown said. "It's life. It's business. Move on. It's OK. … It's emotions. You've got to pack up and move your stuff. Your family's got to pack up and move. But it's life. It's happened to numerous amounts of people, myself included.

"So I just look forward to being able to build with my team over here, trying to make the best of my situation."

Brown faced the Celtics during the preseason, but Tuesday will be the first meeting that counts. He said he did not know whether that would introduce emotions that were absent during the exhibition schedule.

"We'll see how I feel when we get out there," Brown said. "As of now, I've got no emotion toward nothing. So I'm just ready to go."

Tatum was similarly restrained after Boston's 117-97 victory over Milwaukee on Sunday.

"It's another game on the schedule," Tatum said. "It's the Celtics versus the Sixers. In the words of Brad Stevens, they all count as one."

Tatum called the Sixers "an incredible group" with a "great coaching staff," but declined to add to the discussion surrounding his relationship with Brown.

"Drama gets clicks and views," he said. "That's not my thing." CBS Sports reported Tatum's full comments Sunday night.

Celtics head coach Joe Mazzulla was even more concise.

"It'll be fun," Mazzulla said. "It'll be a good atmosphere."

Brown is not the only centerpiece of the July deal who will face his former team.

George has settled quickly into Boston's lineup, averaging 20.4 points and supplying the high-volume outside shooting the Celtics have long prioritized. He scored 27 points and made five three-pointers Sunday as Boston beat Milwaukee for its third consecutive victory.

The Celtics have not needed George to duplicate everything Brown provided. He has fit alongside Tatum, Derrick White and Boston's returning core as another willing shooter and versatile defender.

Tatum is averaging 27.1 points, six rebounds and 7.8 assists. His playmaking has been especially important for a team that replaced Brown's downhill pressure with more shooting and ball movement.

Boston enters Tuesday ranked third in offensive rating and fourth in defensive rating. It is one of two NBA teams currently inside the top five on both ends of the floor.

The Celtics have also made 15.6 three-pointers per game, the second-highest average in the league. They set an NBA Cup record by hitting 24 against Charlotte on Friday, then made 18 more Sunday in Milwaukee.

That presents a clear challenge for a Sixers defense that has generally performed well but has occasionally surrendered clean perimeter looks after committing extra help toward the ball. Tatum, George, White, Payton Pritchard and Sam Hauser can all punish late rotations.

The Sixers have reached 9-2 without depending upon the same formula every night.

Tyrese Maxey leads them at 26.9 points per game. Brown is averaging 22.3 points, 6.7 rebounds and four assists while shooting 47.7 percent from the field and 39.2 percent from three. Joel Embiid and LeBron James have supplied scoring, passing and defensive flexibility around them.

Brown has repeatedly described the team as unfinished despite its record.

"From our perspective, it's been some growing pains for sure, even though we've been able to win," he said. "The result, we're not so much fixated on the result. It's more so about the process, and we've got to improve our process and our mentality to creating a winning environment."

Boston should provide the clearest early measure of that process.

The Celtics play considerably slower than the Sixers, but their half-court execution has been more efficient. Philadelphia will want to create turnovers and run before Boston can set its defense. The Celtics will attempt to control the pace, generate three-pointers and make the Sixers execute against a defense that has yet to allow an opposing player to score 30 points this season.

There is also the unavoidable Brown matchup. Boston could use Tatum, George and White against him at different points, giving the Celtics several defenders capable of absorbing his drives without immediately sending help.

Nick Nurse expects Brown to bring something extra, even if Brown has publicly downplayed the emotional component.

"It's going to be very different for him," Nurse said. "I think probably a little bit of emotion in there, too, but also I think he's going to be very motivated to play."

Boston's rotation is healthy entering the matchup. Two-way guard Devin Carter is out because of an illness.

Dominick Barlow and Ariel Hukporti remain out for the Sixers. Adem Bona could return after missing Sunday's contest.

The betting market views the matchup as essentially a toss-up. Monday afternoon listings had the spread moving between Boston minus-1.5 and Philadelphia minus-1.5, depending on the sportsbook, with the total at 215.5.

That fits the larger picture. Philadelphia owns the East's best record. Boston has the conference's strongest point differential and has outscored opponents by 10.7 points per game.

The surrounding conversation will center on Brown and Tatum until the opening tip. Brown does not intend to contribute any ceremonial small talk.

"He's on his side," Brown said. "I'm on my side."

#2931. Five good Texans questions with

This week marks the second Deshaun Watson revenge game of the series. Of course, one could be excused if they aren't quite sure who wants the revenge more. More importantly, both the Houston Texans and Cleveland Browns are in the playoff hunt in the AFC. Naturally, the Browns are in slightly better shape, but neither can afford a loss at this point. We caught up with Chris Pokorny of Dawgs by Nature to see what we could figure out about the Browns before Sunday's game.

Battle Red Blog: The Browns have been better this season under their new coaching staff. What have they been doing differently than the old Stefanski regime? Is this something that is sustainable throughout the season?

Chris Pokorny: Although it hasn't worked as blissfully the past couple weeks, I always harp on the fact that this new coaching staff seems willing to make a lot more adjustments than the old coaching staff. I thought Kevin Stefasnki was really effective when he had an offensive line that could dominate games with a zone-blocking running scheme, and that was complemented by an aggressive Jim Schwartz defense. But over time, when the linemen were injured and past their prime, there were no adjustments to re-invent the offense; and on defense, Schwartz stuck with the same approach, even if a team 'figured out' out to combat it. Under Todd Monken, at least on defense, you get the feeling that there is a different gameplan and ability to make adjustments each week. On offense, Monken has been more willing to fully embrace all of his young players with boatloads of playing time. It's been fun to see the Browns be competitive, but it's also concerning that they haven't had at least one game — despite an 'easy' schedule — in which they've been able to control the game with a decisive victory.

BRB: The Browns appear poised to go back to Deshaun Watson after trying Shadeur Sanders. How do the two quarterbacks differ and do you think Watson gives the Browns a better chance to win?

CP: When you look at his stats in last week's game against the Saints, it looked like Shedeur Sanders did great. But if you look at things from a strategic gameplan perspective, needing to make reads, being faced with that key third- or fourth-down situation earlier in a game, Sanders still appears way to slow to process things at the NFL level. Where he succeeded was later in the game, when it was do-or-die, and the 'gameplan' almost goes out the window because he's chucking it deep as he takes big hits. It worked, and honestly, that has been his strength throughout his NFL starts. It's exciting, and I think it shows he can make the big throws at this level, but it's also not something that is sustainable. He needs to mix that in with a much higher degree of competency on the scripted plays throughout a game, and he's not there yet. Deshaun Watson was poor at that since signing with the Browns too, looking very skittish, but this year he's been tremendously better at that.

3) The Browns have been a team in transition the last few seasons. Who are some new faces this year that will have a dramatic impact on the game on Sunday?

They are no secret, but rookie receivers Denzel Boston and KC Concepcion lead their class in receiving yards. I can only imagine how good these guys can be when they have an upper-tier quarterback throwing their way, they are an exciting duo for fans to look forward to for years to come. On defense, I wish I could call out Jared Verse's name as the replacement to Myles Garrett, but Verse has been a huge disappointment, ranking near the bottom of the league in metrics. I'd say that linebacker Quincy Williams, who came over from the Jets, has had a nice impact on the defense, with a few games where he borders on looking like an elite-level player (but that's not a week-in, week-out thing).

BRB: The AFC North is usually one of the more competitive divisions in the NFL. Who do you see winning the division this season? Which team in the division do Browns fans enjoy beating the most?

CP: A few weeks ago, I would have thought the Browns had a solid shot since they were 2-0 in the division, had a 5-2 record, and the Ravens were beat up at quarterback. But the Steelers have shown a greater degree of consistency that I didn't anticipate, and even though I think the Bengals' defense has been overrated, Joe Burrow is playing at a high level, so there's no reason to think those teams will fade. I think the Steelers' running game being so good will allow them to win the division, along with their defense picking up the intensity. The Browns and Bengals should be contending for a wildcard spot along with the Patriots (and maybe the Texans if they gain steam). Regarding which team Browns fans enjoy beating the most in the division, I'd say it's still the Steelers. We were so looking forward to ending our 22-game road losing streak in Cleveland a few weeks ago, but Pittsburgh blew us out there again.

BRB: The Texans are favored by 4.5 points according to Fanduel. How do you see the game going? Are there any prop bets you feel comfortable recommending?

CP: Especially with the Browns finally in contention at midseason for once, I'm really trying to stay optimistic with my picks each week, so keep that in mind here. The Browns have played a lot of close games, and are 3-0 at home, so this four-game home stretch that they are beginning is vital for them to stay in the playoff race. The Browns' pass defense has remained a strong suit, but offensively, they still have some consistency issues, with too many dry stretches of scoring. Of all the games the Browns have played this season, this is the one where I feel like I have no idea what to expect. C.J. Stroud seems to be playing at an unexpectedly high level, but the Browns' pass defense has been solid. Will Houston commit to the run, which Cleveland hasn't been able to stop for weeks? And while Houston's defense has struggled compared to my expectations, the talent seems to still be there, so do they get back on track against their former quarterback who takes a lot of sacks? I'll take the Browns to win 24-21. As for prop bets, I'll say RB Dylan Sampson over 21.5 yards receiving is a good one. He's hit that mark in all three weeks that he's been back from injury.

We want to thank Chris for taking the time to answer our questions about the Browns. They are certainly one of the more interesting teams in the NFL so far on the season. We want to wish Chris and the Browns the best of luck for the remainder of the season. As per usual, we hope that luck begins on Monday morning.

#2927. Top Wall Street analysts find these 3 stocks attractive as long-term investments

Investors have been grappling with stock market volatility, weighing elevated oil prices, persistent geopolitical tensions, the Federal Reserve's rate hike and a debate about AI safety. For all that, it helps to look beyond short-term noise and focus on stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors gain clarity, as these experts conduct in-depth research to pick stocks with attractive growth prospects.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Enterprise software and cloud infrastructure company Oracle (ORCL) recently announced better-than-expected results for the first quarter of fiscal 2027. The quarter benefited from solid growth in cloud infrastructure revenue.

After hosting a series of investor meetings with Ken Bond, Oracle head of investor relations following Q1 earnings, Guggenheim analyst John DiFucci reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $400. Oracle remains a Guggenheim Best Idea, he said.

"We continue to believe ORCL is a decade stock, driven by the massive (and profitable) opportunity ahead for AI training and inferencing," said DiFucci. He also cited the traditional Public Cloud business and additional AI-enabled cloud database and cloud applications for his bullish stance. DiFucci expects all the factors to drive revenue and profit acceleration over the next few years.

While Oracle delivered 850 MW of data center capacity in Q1 FY27, Bond cautioned that Infrastructure as a Service revenue in any quarter depends more on the timing of when that capacity comes online, than on the headline MW number. DiFucci expects IaaS growth to stay strong, with full-year growth expected above Q1 FY27's 120% constant currency rate. Management expects total revenue growth to accelerate sequentially throughout fiscal 2027, as the faster-growing infrastructure business makes up a larger share of the mix.

DiFucci addressed concerns regarding customer concentration, saying OpenAI's share of total remaining performance obligations has dropped from 80% last June to about 50% now, and is expected to keep falling as new contracts are signed with new and existing customers.

DiFucci ranks No. 263 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 62% of the time, delivering an average return of about 17.5%. See Oracle Insider Trading Activity on TipRanks.

Space systems and launch services provider Rocket Lab (RKLB) was recently initiated as a buy with an $80 price target by Raymond James analyst Brian Gesuale.

"RKLB has assembled launch, spacecraft, components, payloads and optical communications, with Neutron and Iridium completing medium-lift and applications/spectrum in 2027," said Gesuale.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that with the completion of the Iridium acquisition, Rocket Lab will become the only vertically integrated space company with positive free cash flow (FCF). The deal will add a deployed constellation valued at more than $3 billion, globally coordinated L-band spectrum and about $500 million in earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization (EBITDA) and $300 million in free cash flow (FCF).

Gesuale highlighted RKLB's strong revenue and backlog growth and progress toward positive EBITDA and FCF in 2027/2028. Rocket Lab is moving from a heavy investment phase into monetization, with estimated revenue growth of 59% in 2026, the analyst said, noting that RKLB's backlog has more than doubled to almost $2.4 billion from $1.1 billion in Q3 2025.

Given strong growth projections, the analyst expects RKLB's gross margins to expand from about a 35% trough in 2027 to about 47% by 2030, while R&D spending is estimated to decline from ~35% of sales in 2026 to about 10% in 2028, creating substantial operating leverage as the platform matures.

Gesuale ranks No. 369 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of about 17%. See Rocket Lab Statistics on TipRanks.

J.P. Morgan analyst Doug Anmuth recently upgraded Meta Platforms (META) to buy from hold and raised his price target to $820 from $640. Despite the year-to-date underperformance of META, the 5-star analyst still sees significant upside, as Meta is in the early stages of launching frontier models and AI-powered offerings beyond advertising, particularly the Muse AI agent and Meta Model API access.

"Frontier models are at the core of Meta's product and monetization pipeline over a multi-year period, and its path toward superintelligence," said Anmuth.

In the summer of 2025, Meta set a goal to rebuild its Meta Superintelligence Labs team and deliver frontier AI models within a year, the analyst said. The team appears to have met its goal, rapidly advancing from the rollout of Muse Spark 1.1 in July to Muse Spark 1.3, which is competitive with Claude and GPT models, Anmuth said.

Meanwhile, Meta's Watermelon product will expand opportunities in consumer products, business intelligence, and Family of Apps engagement, the analyst said. The product will enhance internal operations and overall efficiency, said Anmuth, who's confident of Meta's ability to bring consumer-driven AI products to its extensive user base of 4 billion and views its distribution network as a competitive advantage.

While Meta has focused on frontier models and AI product development, Anmuth also sees solid opportunity in its AI infrastructure capacity, which he believes will support future compute needs.

Anmuth ranks No. 832 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of about 9.40%. See Meta Platforms Financials on TipRanks.

#2926. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

#2925. Celtics Mailbag: Legit contenders? Areas to improve, and more

Celtics Mailbag: Legit contenders? Areas to improve, and more originally appeared on NBC Sports Boston

It's been a frenetic and emotional — at least after all the hype in the build-up to Tuesday's visit to see old friend Jaylen Brown and the Philadelphia 76ers  — start to the 2026-27 season for the Boston Celtics.

Still, Boston arrived at its first extended break of the new campaign with an Eastern Conference-best 10-2 record. So how should we feel about that?

That was the biggest question when we asked a bunch of our Celtics group chats to send over their biggest queries through the first 12 games of the season.

So let's rip open the Celtics Mailbag for the first time this season. We'll get into what the two losses exposed, what Brad Stevens might actually need to shop for, and what the headband colors are telling us.

But we start with the question that gets a little harder to brush off with every win:

Have the Celtics established themselves as legitimate contenders through 12 games?

Look, it's probably too early to make wholesale declarations about what this team is capable of when the postseason arrives. Even after dealing away Brown, many pundits suspected the Celtics would be a very good regular-season team. But it's hard to argue with the early season numbers, including …

Boston is 10-2 overall, best record in the East

Ranks top 10 in defensive (second, 105.6) and offensive (sixth, 117.4) rating

A +12.2 point differential suggests a team on a 67-win pace

Boston ranks 26th in strength of schedule

The best teams each season tend to take care of business at home and feast on inferior competition. You can only play the schedule as it's drawn up. We'd feel better if Boston hadn't fumbled away a 22-point lead in Detroit on opening night, or shown a little bit better in Houston despite playing on the second night of a back-to-back.

Basically, we'd sum it up like this: If you said the Celtics were going to be 10-2 after 12 games to start the season, particularly with three sets of back-to-backs and some crazy early season travel, we'd probably have taken it. Boston has only scratched the surface of its full potential, but there's some very encouraging early trends, including Jayson Tatum's play, Paul George's availability, and positive contributions throughout the supporting cast.

Can we revisit the contender/pretender talk around Christmas?

What should be on Brad Stevens' early-season shopping list?

We've seen a lot of way-too-early chatter about Boston's trade needs, probably from people who put their Christmas tree up on November 1. We're not judging you, but we're kinda judging you.

One thing we do think is really important to keep in mind when we get closer to December 15 and the unofficial start of NBA trade season: The Celtics have heavy motivation to remain under the luxury tax for a second straight season, which would reset pesky repeater penalties and give Stevens an opportunity to potentially splurge a bit next summer.

The Celtics currently sit less than $2 million below the tax line, meaning something as simple as filling the vacant 15th roster spot would stress their budget. There's still pathways to bigger tinkering, but it would likely require sending out some of Boston's non-minimum salaries to add an impact player.

We do think that, given the glut of wing talent and only so many minutes to go around, even in instances where the Celtics prioritize rest for veteran players, there should be consideration of consolidating if a particular need exists elsewhere on the roster closer to February.

The roster, otherwise, is about as balanced as the Celtics have been in recent seasons. We wouldn't suggest there's an obvious need through 12 games. Some will lust for players that either 1) Put pressure on the rim or 2) Can simply take stress off the Tatum/George combo. Alas, those players routinely command salaries that Boston would struggle to absorb, even if willing to move on from core talent.

The bigger priority right now should be getting Mitchell Robinson comfy here, and figuring out the youngest players the team should lean into.

The Celtics have heavy motivation to remain under the luxury tax for a second straight season.

Did Detroit and Houston expose anything that Boston needs to get better at?

Good teams narrow your margin for error. So the Celtics might be able to survive their miscues and steal an overtime win against the Nets, but lose your way for six minutes against Detroit, then you've fumbled away a 22-point lead and the game.

We could playfully distill it down to Boston thriving whenever there's not a Thompson twin on the other side of the court. But what that probably suggests is that, when there are defenders that can challenge Tatum, it certainly puts a greater stress on the supporting cast to shoulder the scoring load.

In those instances, the Celtics need Payton Pritchard and Derrick White to be great, even against teams that might have the size and physicality to make both guards uncomfortable.

That said, Boston acquitted itself well in wins over New York and Philly, two teams with incredibly skilled starting fives. The schedule gets a bit more challenging through the end of November, though Boston might benefit from the fact that opponents will be dealing with some schedule difficulty or managing injuries.

A little end-of-the-month NBA Cup showdown with Atlanta should be fun, and there's a visit to Wemby and the Spurs right after. If the Celtics qualify for Cup quarterfinals (or beyond), we'll learn a bit more about how this team fares against top-level competition.

If you could lead the league in "being in the right spot on both ends of the floor," Baylor Scheierman would be among the league leaders.

If a playoff series started tomorrow, which young wing would you trust most?

The answer here is Baylor Scheierman. One of the stories we have in draft mode is basically how even with all these crazy analytics we pour over every day, we still can't properly quantify Scheierman's impact. If you could lead the league in "being in the right spot on both ends of the floor," we think he'd be among the league leaders. If you could quantify a higher-level hustle play — breaking up an alley-oop attempt with a deflection out of bounds, or starting a fast break by passing from the floor after recovering a loose ball — then Scheierman's stat line would pop a bit more. His basketball IQ is simply off the charts, and it makes him super impactful even when he's not shooting (though his 3-point percentage might beg that he hoist even a few more).

Hugo Gonzalez has had his moments. He was insanely active in that Philadelphia game. He's defending 22 shots per 100 possessions when he's on the court, which ranks in the 99th percentile for his position. Opponents are shooting 7.1 percent below expected on the shots he does defend, one of the best marks among all Boston players. You can make a strong case that Boston should continue to accelerate Gonzalez's development by carving out even more minutes for him.

Jordan Walsh has benefited from his starting minutes, and the confidence is growing on the offensive end. The Celtics have a real luxury with their wing depth but, when the games really matter in the postseason, we'd probably yearn to see Scheierman and Gonzalez dominating those minutes.

Who's getting to the rim when Jayson Tatum sits?

The Celtics rank dead last in the NBA with only 51.1 drives and 25 drive points generated per game. This is not wholly unexpected. You trade away Jaylen Brown, and you've got Tatum still learning to trust his body to attack the basket, and that number was always going to project low with a roster built heavy on 3-point shooting.

What it does demand is that, regardless of opponent, the Celtics need Payton Pritchard in attack mode. He's second on the team with 11.8 drives per game (Tatum is at 14.5). The encouraging number: Pritchard is shooting 46.7 percent on 3.8 field goal attempts off drives per game, and generating 1.2 assists off drives per game. The Celtics simply need him and Derrick White (8.8 drives per game) to put pressure on the rim regardless of Tatum's on-court status. White seems to have found his floater, which is a very encouraging sign.

The Celtics don't need to drive just to drive. But we know how important paint touches can be to creating opportunities for shooters. It's something to monitor, especially if the team downshifts and settles for perimeter looks when the offense sputters. That's when they need to embrace attack mode a bit more.

Can Paul George be the No. 2 on a contending team at age 36?

George is putting together an All-Defense-caliber season as one of the league leaders in deflections and steals. He's 11th in the East in scoring at 22 points per game. Would it be wild to suggest he's an early All-Star candidate based on Boston's hot start and the way his two-way impact has helped fuel it?

We'll see how his body holds up over 82 games. George surely was one of the best players on the floor when Philly rallied out of a 3-1 series hole against Boston last season, though the late-game suspension likely aided his body in being ready for that grind. It's slightly ambitious to ask him to do that over multiple rounds.

The positive outlook would be that the Celtics know full well how to bring their veterans along. Even if he's not on the full Al Horford plan in terms of rest, the Celtics will give George every opportunity to keep the body fresh over the regular-season grind.

Slice up the hierarchy however you like, but it's clear that everyone behind Tatum needs to be locked in and consistent for this team to thrive against high-level competition.

Paul George has been outstanding so far for the C's.

Biggest concern(s) through the early portion of the Celtics schedule?

We're admittedly nitpicking here given the strong start, but it's pretty undeniable that the Celtics have to figure out how to get the best version of Mitchell Robinson on the floor.

Whether Robinson is simply ramping himself up after New York's long championship season, or if it's simply navigating some bumps in the acclimation process, Robinson just hasn't been as impactful as even in smaller bursts through camp and preseason.

The fact that Neemias Queta and Luka Garza have both put up some of the best on-court numbers for the team so far this season has given the team some ability to figure things out with Robinson. Teammates gotta get better at lobs — though they've looked good when they work. We'd like to see Robinson crank up the motor and create post-up opportunities in transition like we've seen in small glimpses. We have to think he won't smoke as many close-range putbacks as he has through the early portion of the schedule.

It goes back to the overarching conversation here: The Celtics are built to be regular-season monsters. The question is, how do they transition that success and thrive in the postseason?

Some of that might simply be Tatum being great on the big stage. Some of it is making sure that players in the top six of the rotation — from Robinson to White to Pritchard — are playing their best basketball on that stage, too. There's a lot of time to work it all out.

Nerdiest stat in your spreadsheet to start the season?

The Celtics have worn white headbands in 10 of their 12 games. The green and black headbands are undefeated (1-0 apiece), but the white headband has brought out the best individual numbers from players like Scheierman. The NBA now mandates that players all wear the same color headband, so we suspect the colorful ones might be a bit rarer for players this season.

#2923. 2026 NFL QB Rankings: Midseason Edition

We are roughly halfway through the season, and it's time to update our QB rankings once again. I thought this season was going to be a fascinating season to rank quarterbacks because I felt that the back half of the top 10 was wildly in flux. I felt that the top 5 was pretty well established, and with Father Time catching up to Matthew Stafford, there's even a spot in the top 5 up for grabs.

But the biggest news is that we have knocked the King from the top spot. Patrick Mahomes has always been first on this list over the last two seasons, but it's time to acknowledge a fact: Josh Allen is now the best QB in the NFL.

Here's a list of the updated rankings, and as a reminder, these rankings are my opinion based on how the QB is playing this year, but also looking at their full body of work. Justin Herbert is not one of the top 30 QBs this season in isolation, but it's tough to rank him that low based on his previous seasons of success.

The King is dead. Mahomes is still great, but he doesn't impact the game as Allen does, and Allen has been at this level for the last couple of years. It's time to make the switch up top.

I think many of us keep wondering if we are ever going to get back to that peak Mahomes 2018 to 2022 version. He seems to be gone, but the 2023-2026 version is still very, very good.

Burrow is headed towards his first MVP, and it's one he absolutely deserves. Elite play without question.

In 2024, Lamar had 45 touchdowns and 4 INTs. That wasn't that long ago, but the injuries have slowed him down significantly the last two seasons. If Lamar has another injury-riddled season next year, he may start tumbling in 2027.

Brock Purdy has earned this spot with his play, but I also don't think he's truly a top 5 QB at the NFL level. Top 10? Yes. But I don't think Purdy is in this tier, and there are guys biting at his heels that could easily jump him before the 2026 rankings are finalized in January.

Caleb's play from week one and his three-game stretch returning from injury was better than anyone else's in the league. But 4 INTs, even if it's a blip, certainly has to be considered, and between that and the injury, it keeps Caleb out of the top 5 (for now).

I've been fairly critical of Love, but it's hard to argue with what he is doing with a suspect offensive line and no running game. The Packers have the best deep-ball passing attack in the league, and it's not particularly close.

After what Lawrence has done in the back half of last season and the first half of this season with Liam Coen, he has cemented Lawrence as a top-10 QB; now it's time to see how far he can climb.

Dak has led a maddeningly inconsistent and underperforming offense. He's still an excellent player, but he's most accurately considered a borderline top-10 QB, and that's where he's sitting midway through the season.

Stafford's season has been fine, but it has vibes quite similar to Tom Brady's final season in Tampa. The numbers are down across the board. They're still good, but not MVP-level like last year, and you have to wonder if this might be Father Time knocking at the door.

There may be nobody better operating from a clean pocket than Goff. Unfortunately, there are many QBs that operate far better under pressure. If Goff ever managed chaos well, he could climb as high as the top 5, but at this point in his career, that's probably not happening.

Patriots fans will probably see this ranking and be annoyed, but until Maye starts performing at a much better level against better teams, for me, it's going to be hard to push him into those elite spots on the rankings.

He's playing the best football since his rookie season, and he's leading a Texans team that we thought would be carried by their defense. If the Texans make the playoffs, it's going to be behind Stroud's right arm.

Daniels still shows flashes of who he was as a rookie, but a messy offense, injuries, and really poor decisions late in games are keeping Daniels down. He's going to need to string together a solid couple of months of football while staying healthy if he ever wants to get out of this middle group.

I still don't trust Nix, but maybe I should start. When the game is on the line, Nix makes the plays; he just doesn't do it for the first 50 to 55 minutes of the game. If Nix starts putting together a more complete game on a consistent basis, he can surge towards the top 10.

Herbert entered the season with a chance to finally break into the top 5 with a big season; instead, he's playing at a level that makes him the worst starting QB in the NFL. Tough to drop him all the way down with his history of regular season success, but he's playing at a level that nobody can consider him one of the league's top 10 QBs until he proves otherwise.

Darnold is another guy who had a chance to start changing the narrative around his name after two great seasons and a Super Bowl championship, but he has struggled this year and arguably looked worse than his backup, Drew Lock. If Seattle is going to get back into the playoff hunt, Darnold needs to return to form.

It's tough to have Hurts much higher than this point when the Eagles are clearly stocking up to potentially replace him. Hurts being out for the year with blood clots is awful for him, but based on where Hurts was prior to the issue, the writing does seem to be on the wall here in Philly.

Young had really started stringing together some good games where you had to potentially consider him being in the top half of the league's QBs, but in the last three games, he has 5 TDs, 4 INTs, has completed less than 60% of his passes, and a Y/A of 6.1. He's reminded us who he is as a QB.

Shough had a brutal game against the Vikings but has been pretty darn good in all his other games. He needs to start leading the Saints to some wins (which he could) down the stretch to start pushing his way out of the 20s.

Cousins has done a nice job winding the clock back and playing some pretty darn good football. He doesn't have a high ceiling in Minnesota, but he does have a much higher one than their other QBs, and he helps this Vikings team tremendously.

Rodgers isn't playing as well as some are hyping him up to be, but he's playing a lot better than most thought he could. He's quite limited at this point, but he can still deliver the football when he needs to.

Dart awaits his chance next year to show that he's ready to leap an NFL QB.

Baker is certainly showing signs of slipping. He's 31, has been dealing with injuries, and doesn't look like the guy Tampa has had the last three years. The Baker extension that he signed in September may age like warm milk for Tampa.

I think the question for this point with Penix is whether he is truly a low-end QB1, or a high-end QB2. He's the best QB on Atlanta's roster, but that QB room doesn't exactly have Joe Montana and Steve Young in it.

Geno is playing much better this year in New York than he did last year in Vegas, but he's still quite limited and not going to be anything more than a bridge QB for whatever team he's with.

Brissett lost his job in Arizona and is now going to try and keep the Ravens afloat while they hope Lamar can get back in December. Brissett has shown he's an excellent QB2 but really shouldn't be starting anywhere, and I'm curious if this is who Penix ends up becoming.

Watson continues to play better than what many thought he was capable of at this point in his career, but it's also safe to say that Cleveland is winning despite Watson, not because of him. Cleveland needs to find something better at QB; if they do, Monken could really take off in Cleveland.

Jones is the best QB2 in the NFL. He hasn't needed to play this year, but when he had to fill in for Purdy last year, he ran Shanahan's system fantastically. It'll be curious if Jones gets a chance somewhere to be a starter again in the next couple of years.

This is probably a little too high for Jameis, but when pressed into duty, he's been giving the Giants a lot more good than bad. It'll be curious what the Giants do next year. His contract is guaranteed, but they'll have JJ McCarthy and Jaxson Dart as well.

The Titans offense isn't exactly a juggernaut, but they have a pretty good offensive line, Tony Pollard, Carnell Tate, and a couple of other decent weapons. It's definitely getting to the point where the Titans have to wonder if Ward was the right call at QB. He just isn't taking that next step.

Murray was a better option for Minnesota than Carson Wentz or JJ McCarthy, but there's a reason they went out and got Kirk Cousins. The team wasn't going anywhere with Murray as their QB1.

Lock has really played well, all things considered, for Seattle this year. He's won all 3 games that he's had to play; meanwhile, Darnold is 2-4 when he's under center. Lock has established himself as one of the top 5 QB2s in the league.

It made plenty of sense for the Dolphins to give Willis a shot, but he's not the answer at QB. The Dolphins are going to have a top-5 pick (maybe first overall), and they'll need to look long and hard at the QBs available.

He has officially turned back into a pumpkin. The Giants gave Jones a big contract, and it blew up in their face. Those who do not learn from history are doomed to repeat it. And that's why Chris Ballard doesn't have a job.

Burrow has been as good as anyone in the league, but he does have a tendency to get injured, and having a capable backup like Flacco is a great spot for Cincinnati if he's called upon.

It's time to see what Mendoza can do and have an actual idea of where he should actually be ranked on this list.

Another very capable QB2 who unfortunately has joined Lamar Jackson on IR. It would have been interesting to see how Huntley would look in extended time.

Mariota has settled nicely into his role as QB2. With a guy like Daniels who can easily get banged up, like the Bengals, Mariota is a nice guy to fall back on.

Sanders definitely looked better than Watson did in his opportunity. It'll be curious to see if Monken makes the switch in the next couple of weeks to see if Sanders can spark the Browns, who have lost two straight and 3 of their last 5.

Wentz's performance on Monday Night Football was one of the worst by a QB we've ever seen. He's lucky he's still this high on the list.

McCarthy has certainly looked better in New York than he did in Minnesota, but we need to slow down on the praise. His overall numbers still aren't great, and he threw a crippling interception to end Thursday Night Football. He still isn't showing that he's a starting-caliber QB.

Daniels has played better this season than Mayfield; not sure if that's praise for a UDFA rookie QB or criticism for a veteran QB who just got handed a second extension from Tampa. Either way, he remains a really nice story this season.

Minshew probably should have been in the top 50 despite not really playing because he is exactly what you need out of your QB2. He had a nice moment in Arizona, but his season is over due to injury.

Remember when Davis Mills hit the game-winning touchdown for Lovie Smith and that gave the Bears the first pick in the 2023 draft, which led to the Bryce Young trade, which led to the Bears drafting Caleb Williams? Good times.

Keenum still rides on the list thanks to his performance against the Eagles this year. It's still one of the feel-good stories of the year.

I still think Taylor is a solid QB2 even if he hasn't seen the field for the Packers.

Bagent is still clinging to his top 50 spot on the list, but it won't take much to bump him off with guys like Justin Fields tumbling off the list completely; Bagent could easily be next.

I would suspect Mitch is actually a better QB than Cam Ward is at this moment, but the Titans need to see if Ward can become their guy, so Mitch continues to hold the clipboard and be a veteran presence in the QB room.

#2921. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guidance; Extension of Comment Period (PDF)

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guide for Traditional Community Banking Organizations (PDF)

Agencies seek comment on proposed third-party risk management guidance and issue statement on community bank engagement with core service providers

#2917. Game Preview: San Antonio Spurs vs Golden State Warriors

Left for dead by plenty of pundits over the summer, the Golden State Warriors came storming out of the gates this season, led by the somehow still otherworldly play of Steph Curry and the surprising play of rookie small forward Yaxel Lendeborg. They were riding an 8-game win streak before losing both their match-up with the Houston Rockets on Wednesday night and Curry, who suffered an oblique abdominal tear just 6 minutes into that contest, never returning. Curry, who was averaging 30 points on ridiculous efficiency in the lead up to the Houston game, is out for at least the next 2 weeks, leaving the Warriors without the engine that drives their entire offense. Golden State to their credit hung with Houston most of the night thanks to Lendeborg's shotmaking, a focused team defensive effort, and offensive contributions from both vets and young guys alike.

Coming into this one on a 10 game winning streak, the San Antonio Spurs have been taking care of business. Facing Golden State without Curry makes them that much easier to guard, but head coach Steve Kerr's defensive game planning, Draymond Green's orchestration both on offense and defense, and Lendeborg's breakout is the sort of combination that could still make life rough on their opponents. San Antonio was able to sleepwalk their way into a big deficit before their huge comeback back win against the Chicago Bulls Wednesday night in Chicago, but against the well-coached Warriors that could be a recipe for disaster.

San Antonio Spurs (10-2) vs Golden State Warriors (8-4)November 13 2026 | 8:30 PM CTWatch: Amazon Prime | Listen: WOAI (1200 AM)Line: San Antonio -10.5

Spurs Injuries: Tobias Harris, calf (OUT), Devin Vassell, groin (day to day)

Warriors Injuries: Steph Curry, oblique (OUT), Jimmy Butler, ACL (OUT), Moses Moody, knee (OUT), Brandon Williams, hamstring (OUT), Gary Payton II, knee (day to day)

Golden State has been notoriously small for years, but they have just a single player on the roster this season that's taller than 6'8. That should mean open season for Victor Wembanyama, who has been on a historical tear to kick off his 4th season in the league and is coming off another huge performance in the win over Chicago. Draymond Green is still a versatile defender with an elite IQ on the defensive side of the ball, but at age 36 and in his 15th season, he is no longer the perennial DPOY-candidate he once was in his prime that could make life difficult on opposing bigs for significant stretches. Regardless though, expect him to draw a decent amount of the assignment on Wemby with help sprinkled in from Golden State's wings. Green and Wembanyama had a notable moment between them last season at the Frost Bank Center almost exactly a year to the day.

With Chef Curry out for the foreseeable future, the bulk of the offense could fall on the shoulders of rookie Yaxel Lendeborg. Lendeborg has been a revelation for Golden State, averaging 21.7 points, 7.4 rebounds, and 3.7 assists on 51% shooting from the field and 43% from 3 on solid volume. An older rookie at 24 years old, he's proven to be adept at attacking closeouts and getting into the teeth of the defense, using his big body (6'9" with a nearly 7'4" wingspan) to go at smaller defenders in the post, and seeking out contact and either finishing through it or finding his way to the charity stripe (6 attempts a game at the line). Opposing defenses have been preoccupied with Curry's offensive blitz to start the season and leaving Lendeborg as the #2 option, but that obviously changes with Steph out now. San Antonio has a variety of defenders they can and will send at him to see how he holds up as the focus of the defense. Should all else fail, they have the always lurking Wembanyama as the ultimate insurance policy.

Golden State has been among the league's most proficient 3 point shooting teams to begin this season, ranking 1st in both makes and percentage. That's largely (and obviously) been because of Curry's volume and consistency from distance, but it's not just about the numbers he puts up. It's also because of the sort of looks that his gravity is able to open up for others. Without him to warp help defenses and open up the easy catch and shoot opportunities for guys like Lendeborg and Brandin Podziemski, their offensive efficiency is likely about to take a hit. That doesn't mean that they still aren't going to launch them though, as they showed with their 17/51 showing in the loss to the Rockets.

If you'd like to, you may follow along with the game on our Twitter profile (@poundingtherock) or visit our Game Thread!
